\label{def:localizedpushforwards} Let $g \colon X \mapsto \Gamma_{\infty}^{r}$ be an $M$-definable map. Let $\lambda_{\nu,g}$ be the Keisler measure obtained on $\mathbb{B}^{\Gamma}_{r}(M)$ given by taking the pushforward of $\mu_{\nu,M}$ along the map $g$. More precisely,
for every $\Gamma(M)$-definable set $E\subseteq\Gamma_\infty^r$, let  $\lambda_{\nu,g}(E):=\mu_{\nu,M}\bigl(X\cap g^{-1}(E)\bigr)$. We refer to $\lambda_{\nu,g}$ as the \emph{localized pushforward of $\mu_{\nu,M}$ along $g$.}
<!-- end definition-18 -->
<!-- fact-11: body_md -->
\label{fact:imageiscountablyadditive} Since the Keisler measure $\mu_{\nu,M}$ on $M$ defined in \cref{def:keislermeasure} is countably additive (\emph{c.f} \cref{lem:local-radon-barycentre-is-measure}), each of the localized pushforwards $\lambda_{\nu,g}$ defined in \cref{def:localizedpushforwards} is also countably additive over $\Gamma_\infty(M)$.
<!-- end fact-11 -->
<!-- definition-19: body_md -->
\label{def:bounded}Let $M \models \mathrm{ACVF}$ and $X \subseteq M^{n}$ an $M$-definable subset in the valued field sort. We say that $X$ is \emph{bounded} if for some $\gamma \in \Gamma(M)$ then $X$ is contained in $\{(x_{1},\dots, x_{n}): v(x_{i})\geq \gamma, 1 \leq i \leq n\}$.  We can extend this notion for $X \subseteq V$ where $V$ is a projective variety defined over $M$. In such case, there are affine open covers $V=\bigcup_{i \leq n} U_{i}$, and we say that $X$ is bounded if there are bounded subsets $X_{i} \subseteq U_{i}$ such that $X=\bigcup_{i\leq n} X_{i}$.
<!-- end definition-19 -->
<!-- fact-12: body_md -->
\label{fact:imageofboundedisbounded} If $X$ is an $M$-definable bounded set and $g: M^{n} \rightarrow \Gamma_{\infty}$ is an $M$-definable function, where $g=v\circ f$ for some regular function $f$ on the affine space $\mathbb{A}^{n}$. Then $V=g(X)$ is always bounded in $\Gamma_{\infty}(M)$.
<!-- end fact-12 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
\label{rmk:quantifiereliminationisthelaw}By quantifier elimination any definable function from some cartesian power of the valued field sort onto $\Gamma_{\infty}$ is piecewise a difference of functions of the form $\frac{1}{n} f-\frac{1}{m}g$ for $f$ and $g$ regular functions on $\mathbb{A}^{n}$ and $n,m \in \mathbb{N}$ (see for example \cite[Section $3.5$]{tamegeo}).
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- fact-13: body_md -->
\label{prop:uniquenessbypushforwards}
Let $\nu$ and $\mu$ be two Kiesler measures over $M$. Assume that for every $M$-definable map $g: M^{n}\rightarrow \Gamma_{\infty}(M)$, the pushforward measures along $g$, $\nu_{g}$ and $\mu_{g}$ agree on $\mathbb{B}^{\Gamma}(\Gamma(M))$. Then $\nu=\mu$. Furthermore, by \cref{rmk:quantifiereliminationisthelaw}, we might as well restrict from all definable functions $g$ to only regular functions.
<!-- end fact-13 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
For $(1)$ by quantifier elimination any definable set $D$ is a boolean combination of formulas of the form $P(x)=0$ and $v(Q_{1}(x)) \leq v(Q(x))$ for $P, Q_{1}(x),Q_{2}(x)$ polynomials with coefficients in $M$. Note the first condition is equivalent to $v(P(x))=\infty$. Take $g=v\circ P$. By hypothesis $\mu\big(\{ x : P(x)=0\}\big)=\mu_{g}\big(\{\infty\}\big)=\nu_{g}\big(\{\infty\}\big)=\nu \big( \{ x : P(x)=0\big).$\\
Similarly, let $g: M^{n}\to \Gamma_{\infty}^{2}(M)$ be the map given by sending $x$ to $(v(Q_{1}(x)),v(Q_{2}(x)))$. Write $D=\{ (\gamma,\beta) \in \Gamma_{\infty}(M)^{2} \ | \ \exists x \in M \ v(P(x))=\gamma \wedge v(Q(x))=\beta\}$. Likewise,\\
$ \mu\big(\{ x : v(Q_{1}(x)) \leq v(Q_{2}(x))\}\big)=\mu_{g}(D)=\nu_{g}(D)=\nu \big(\{ x : v(Q_{1}(x)) \leq v(Q_{2}(x))\}.$
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:radon-barycentre-criterion} Let $M \models \mathrm{ACVF}$ and assume that $\Gamma(M)=(\mathbb R,+, \leq, 0)$. Let $X \subseteq M^{n}$ an $M$-definable set in the valued field sort that is bounded. Let $\nu$ be a Borel probability measure on $\widehat{X}(M)$, equipped with the Hrushovski Loeser topology. Then, for every finite tuple
$e\in\Gamma(\mathbb M)^m$, the measure $\mu_{\nu,M}$ has a unique extension to $Me$, so that every global $M$-invariant extension of
$\mu_{\nu,M}$ is orthogonal to $\Gamma$.  In particular, $\mu_{\nu,M}$ has a
unique global $M$-invariant extension $\mu_\nu$ which is
generically stable over $M$.
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
We start with the following claim.
\begin{claim} The measure $\mu_{v,M}$ has a unique extension for every finite tuple $e \in \Gamma(\mathbb M)^{m}$.
\end{claim}
\begin{claimproof} Fix a finite tuple
$e\in\Gamma(\mathbb M)^m.$
Let $\eta_1$ and $\eta_2$ be two extensions of $\mu_{\nu,M}$ to
$Me$. Let $\phi(x,y)$ be a $\mathcal{L}(M)$ formula where $y$ is a variable ranging in $\Gamma$. Thus  $\varphi(x;e)$ is an arbitrary $\mathcal{L}(Me)$-formula. 
 By \cref{lem:finite-gamma-normal-form}, there are an
$M$-definable set
$S\subseteq\Gamma^r,$ an $M$-definable map
$g:X\mapsto S,$
and a formula $\theta(u;y)$
in the pure language of divisible ordered abelian groups, with
parameters from $\Gamma(M)$, such that
\[\mathbb M \models  \forall x \in X \forall y \in \Gamma^{m} \ \big(  \varphi(x;y)
\leftrightarrow
\theta(g(x);y)\big).\] 
In particular, $\mathbb M \models \forall x \in X \big(\phi(x,e)\leftrightarrow \theta(g(x),e)\big)$. 
We will now take pushforwards of the measures under $g$. Let $\lambda_{\nu,g}$ be the pushforward of $\mu_{\nu, M}$ under $g$, and let $\lambda_1$ and $\lambda_2$ be the pushforwards of $\eta_1$ and $\eta_2$ under
$g$. Then $\lambda_{\nu,g}$ is a Keisler measure on $\mathbb B^{{\Gamma}}_y (\mathbb R_{\infty})$ and $\lambda_1$ and $\lambda_2$ are Keisler measures on $\mathbb B^{\Gamma}_y (\mathbb R_{\infty}\cup \{e\})$ extending $\lambda_{\nu,g}$. Since $X$ is bounded, by \cref{fact:imageofboundedisbounded} so it is $V=g(X)$. By \cref{fact:imageiscountablyadditive} the measure $\lambda_{g,\nu}$ is a countably additive measure on $V$. By \cref{theo: radon and smooth in R}.(1) we have $\lambda_1=\lambda_2$.

Let
$E_e:=\{g(x)\in S\ | \ \theta(g(x);e) \ \text{and}\ x\in X \}$ hence $\eta_1(\varphi(x;e))
=
\lambda_1(E_e)
=
\lambda_2(E_e)
=
\eta_2(\varphi(x;e)).$
Since $\varphi(x;e)$ was arbitrary, we conclude that
$\eta_1=\eta_2.$
Thus $\mu_{\nu,M}$ has a unique extension to $Me$. \end{claimproof} 

\medskip

 By
\cref{fact:global-invariant-extension}, we can choose a global $M$-invariant extension
$\mu_\nu$ of $\mu_{\nu,M}$. By Claim~$1$ for every finite
tuple $e$ from $\Gamma(\mathbb M)$, the restriction $\mu\upharpoonright_{M}=\mu_{\nu,M}$
has a unique extension to $Me$. Hence, $\mu_\nu$ 
is orthogonal to $\Gamma$.
By \cref{thm:main-generically-stable-measures-acvf}, $\mu_\nu$ is generically stable over $M$, therefore $M$-invariant and finitely satisfiable over $M$. By \cite[Proposition~3.3]{genmeasures}, a generically stable
$M$-invariant measure is the unique global $M$-invariant extension
of its restriction to $M$. So $\mu_\nu$ is the unique
global $M$-invariant extension of $\mu_{\nu,M}$ and it is generically stable, as required.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- claim-5: body_md -->
The measure $\mu_{v,M}$ has a unique extension for every finite tuple $e \in \Gamma(\mathbb M)^{m}$.
<!-- end claim-5 -->
<!-- claimproof-5: body_md -->
Fix a finite tuple
$e\in\Gamma(\mathbb M)^m.$
Let $\eta_1$ and $\eta_2$ be two extensions of $\mu_{\nu,M}$ to
$Me$. Let $\phi(x,y)$ be a $\mathcal{L}(M)$ formula where $y$ is a variable ranging in $\Gamma$. Thus  $\varphi(x;e)$ is an arbitrary $\mathcal{L}(Me)$-formula. 
 By \cref{lem:finite-gamma-normal-form}, there are an
$M$-definable set
$S\subseteq\Gamma^r,$ an $M$-definable map
$g:X\mapsto S,$
and a formula $\theta(u;y)$
in the pure language of divisible ordered abelian groups, with
parameters from $\Gamma(M)$, such that
\[\mathbb M \models  \forall x \in X \forall y \in \Gamma^{m} \ \big(  \varphi(x;y)
\leftrightarrow
\theta(g(x);y)\big).\] 
In particular, $\mathbb M \models \forall x \in X \big(\phi(x,e)\leftrightarrow \theta(g(x),e)\big)$. 
We will now take pushforwards of the measures under $g$. Let $\lambda_{\nu,g}$ be the pushforward of $\mu_{\nu, M}$ under $g$, and let $\lambda_1$ and $\lambda_2$ be the pushforwards of $\eta_1$ and $\eta_2$ under
$g$. Then $\lambda_{\nu,g}$ is a Keisler measure on $\mathbb B^{{\Gamma}}_y (\mathbb R_{\infty})$ and $\lambda_1$ and $\lambda_2$ are Keisler measures on $\mathbb B^{\Gamma}_y (\mathbb R_{\infty}\cup \{e\})$ extending $\lambda_{\nu,g}$. Since $X$ is bounded, by \cref{fact:imageofboundedisbounded} so it is $V=g(X)$. By \cref{fact:imageiscountablyadditive} the measure $\lambda_{g,\nu}$ is a countably additive measure on $V$. By \cref{theo: radon and smooth in R}.(1) we have $\lambda_1=\lambda_2$.

Let
$E_e:=\{g(x)\in S\ | \ \theta(g(x);e) \ \text{and}\ x\in X \}$ hence $\eta_1(\varphi(x;e))
=
\lambda_1(E_e)
=
\lambda_2(E_e)
=
\eta_2(\varphi(x;e)).$
Since $\varphi(x;e)$ was arbitrary, we conclude that
$\eta_1=\eta_2.$
Thus $\mu_{\nu,M}$ has a unique extension to $Me$.
<!-- end claimproof-5 -->
<!-- definition-20: body_md -->
\label{def:hl-topology-stable-completion proj} Let $X$ be an algebraic variety defined over $M$, and let $\mathcal{O}_{X}$ be the sheaf structure of $X$. Let $W\subseteq V$ be an $M$-definable Zariski open subset
and let $f \in \mathcal{O}_{X}(W)$ (i.e. a regular function in the affine case and a regular function on a chart in the projective case). For
$p\in\widehat W(M)$, orthogonality to $\Gamma$ implies that the
pushforward of $p$ under
$x\mapsto v(f(x))$ is an element of $\Gamma_\infty(M).$ We denote
this point by $v(f(p)).$ The topology on $\widehat X(M)$ is the coarsest topology for which all
maps
$v(f)\colon  
\widehat W(M)\mapsto \Gamma_\infty(M)$
are continuous, for all $W$ Zariski open on $X$ and $f \in \mathcal{O}_{X}(W)$. Equivalently, the sets
$\{
p\in\widehat W(M) \ | \ 
v(f(p))\in I
\}_{I,f,W}$
where $I\subseteq\Gamma_\infty(M)$ is open in the order topology, $W\subseteq X$ is Zariski open and $f \in \mathcal{O}_{X}(W)$ form a sub-basis. We refer to this topology as the \emph{Hrushovski-Loeser topology}.
<!-- end definition-20 -->
<!-- fact-14: body_md -->
\label{fact:stable-completion-berkovich}
Assume that $M$ is a spherically complete algebraically closed valued field and that $\Gamma(M)=(\mathbb R,+,\leq, 0).$
Let $X$ be an algebraic variety over $M$. Then there is a canonical
homeomorphism
$\widehat X(M)
\ \simeq\
X^{\mathrm{an}},$
where $X^{\mathrm{an}}$ denotes the Berkovich analytification of $X$.
<!-- end fact-14 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
This is \cite[Lemma~14.1.1]{tamegeo}.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- fact-15: body_md -->
\label{fact: Payne}
    Let $X$ be a quasi-projective variety over a field $K$. Then the Berkovich analytification $X^{\mathrm{an}}$ of $X$ is 
    homeomorphic to the inverse limit $\varprojlim \mathbf{Trop}(X,\iota )$, where the limit is taken over all quasiprojective toric embeddings of $X$. (\emph{c.f.} \cite[Definition $4.1$]{Payne09}).  The homeomorphism is given 
     via the inverse map $\varprojlim \pi_\iota$ where 
     $\varprojlim \pi_\iota$ is a natural proper and continuous map from $X^{\mathrm{an}}$ to $\mathbf{Trop}(X,\iota)$.
<!-- end fact-15 -->
<!-- definition-21: body_md -->
\label{def:affine-tropicalization}
The \emph{standard affine tropicalization map} is the coordinatewise
valuation map $\mathrm{trop}_{\mathrm{aff}}\colon
\mathbb A^n(M)\longrightarrow\mathbb R_\infty^n$ defined by 
$(x_1,\ldots,x_n)
\to
(v(x_1),\ldots,v(x_n))$. 

More generally, let \(M[X]\) be  the coordinate ring of regular functions on $\mathbb A^n$, where $X=(x_{1},\dots, x_{n})$. Let
$\mathbf f=(f_1,\ldots,f_r)$ be a finite tuple of regular functions
on $\mathbb A^n$. Let $Y_{\mathbf{f}}$ be the Zariski open set in which all the functions $f_{i}$ are defined. The tuple $\mathbf f$ determines a morphism
$\iota_{\mathbf f}\colon Y_{\mathbf{f}}\to\mathbb A^r$, given by
$x\mapsto(f_1(x),\ldots,f_r(x))$, and hence an associated
\emph{tropicalization map} $ \mathrm{trop}_{\mathbf f}
\colon  Y_{\mathbf{f}}(M)\to \mathbb R_\infty^r$, given by taking the composition $\mathrm{trop}_{\mathrm{aff}}\circ\iota_{\mathbf f}$.\\
More explicitly,
$\mathrm{trop}_{\mathbf f}(x)
=(v(f_1(x)),\ldots,v(f_r(x)))$.
We define the
\emph{tropicalization of $\mathbb A^n$ with respect to $\iota_{\mathbf f}$} by
$\operatorname{Trop}(\mathbb A^n,\iota_{\mathbf f})
:=
\overline{
\{\mathrm{trop}_{\mathbf f}(x): x\in Y_{\mathbf{f}}(M)\}
},$
where the closure is taken in $\mathbb R_\infty^r$, equipped with the product topology.
<!-- end definition-21 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:babytropicalization}
For any tropicalization $\iota$ the set $\mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb P^n,\iota)$ is definably homeomorphic to a compact  subset of $\mathbb{R}_{\infty}^k$ for some $k$. In particular, it is contained in a bounded (closed) box.
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
By \cite[Theorem 3.4.8]{Berkovich} $(\mathbb P^n)^{\mathrm{an}}$ is compact. And we have a continuous map $\pi_{\iota}: (\mathbb P^n)^{\mathrm{an}} \to \mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb{P}^{n},\iota)$, 
where the image of the simple points in $\mathbb P^n$ is proper and dense in $\mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb{P}^n,\iota)$.  Being a continuous image of a compact space $\mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb{P}^n,\iota)$ is compact. In particular, it is regular as it is compact and Hausdorff space.\\
Recall that the tropical image $\mathrm{Trop}(\mathbb{P}^{n},\iota)$ is defined after fixing a toric variety $Y$ and $\iota: \mathbb{P}^{n} \to Y$ is a closed embedding. By \cite[Example $3.2$]{Payne09}, $\mathrm{Trop}(\mathbb{P}^{n},\iota)$ is the gluing of the $\mathrm{Trop}(U_i,\iota\upharpoonright_{U_i})$ for the standard affine covers $U_i$ of $\mathbb P^n$. Let $T_i=\mathrm{Trop}(U_{i},\iota\upharpoonright_{U_i})\subseteq\mathbb R_\infty^{r_i}.$
The topology on $\mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb{P}^{n},\iota)$, is defined by gluing the tropicalizations $T_{i}$ (see the discussion before \cite[Example $3.2$]{Payne09}). In particular, the $(T_i)_{i \leq s}$ form a finite open cover of $\mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb{P}^{n}, \iota)$.\\
On the other hand, the spaces $T_{i}$ are tropicalization of the affine space, so each of them is generated by regular functions as in \cref{def:affine-tropicalization}. In particular, each of them is definable. Hence each $T_{i}$ is a definable subset of $\mathbb R_\infty^{r_{i}}$,  which inherits a natural subspace topology. Furthermore, their overlaps are definable, and the transition maps come from monomial localizations, so they are also definable homeomorphisms.\\
Hence, $\mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb P^n, \iota)$ is a definable space in the sense of van den Dries \cite[Chapter $10$]{vdD}, with a definable regular topology. By \cite[Theorem 1.8 in Chapter 10]{vdD} we may assume $\mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb{P}^{n},\iota)$ to be a definable subset of $\mathbb{R}_{\infty}^k$ with the order topology for some $k$. Since it is compact, it must be contained in a bounded (closed) box.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- proposition-7: body_md -->
\label{prop:projectivecase} Let $M$ be a spherically complete model of $\mathrm{ACVF}$ whose value group is $(\mathbb{R},+, \leq, 0)$. Let $\mu$ be a global $M$-invariant generically stable measure centered in $\mathbb{P}^{n}$. Then there is a unique Radon measure $\hat{\nu}$ on $(\mathbb{P}^{n})^{an}\cong \widehat{\mathbb{P}^{n}}$ which induces $\mu$ (in the sense of \cref{def:keislermeasure}).
<!-- end proposition-7 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
Let $\mu$ be a generically stable measure concentrating on $\mathbb P^n$. Let $\mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb P^n, \iota)$ be any of the tropicalizations of $\mathbb P^n$ in Payne's inverse limit. By \cref{prop:babytropicalization}, $\mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb P^n, \iota)$ is definably homeomorphic to a compact subset $V_{\iota}$ of $\mathbb{R}_{\infty}^k$ for some $k$, which is therefore contained in a box. The pushforward $\mu_\iota$ of $\mu$ on the $\mathbb{R}_{\infty}$-definable set $V_{\iota}$ is generically stable over $\mathbb{R}_{\infty}$, as it is an $M$-definable pushforward of a generically stable measure. By \cref{fact:acvf-stably-embedded-sorts} the value group is distal and stably embedded, so by \cite[Proposition $2.27$]{distal} $\mu_\iota$ is smooth over $\mathbb{R}_{\infty}$. By \cref{theo: radon and smooth in R}.(2) there is a $\sigma$-additive Borel measure $\Lambda_{\iota}$ on $V(\mathbb{R}_{\infty})$ such that $\Lambda_{\iota}$ induces $\mu_{\iota}$ as a Keisler measure on the $\mathbb{R}_{\infty}$-definable subsets of $V$. Note that the system of measures $\Lambda_\iota$ on $\mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb{P}^{n},\iota)$ is by definition compatible with the inverse limit $\varprojlim \mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb P^n, \iota)$. Indeed, all the measures $\mu_{\iota}$ are obtained as the pushforwards of the same Keisler measure $\mu$ on $M$, and they are compatible with the transition maps, and the induced Borel measure $\Lambda_{\iota}$ are unique by \cref{rmk:uniquenessRadon}.\\
All the spaces $\mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb{P}^{n},\iota)$ are compact and Hausdorff, so Prokhorov's Theorem \cite[Theorem $5.5.1$]{burba9} implies that there is a unique Radon measure $\nu$ on $(\mathbb P^n)^{an}\cong \varprojlim \mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb P^n, \iota)$ such that $\pi_{\iota}(\nu)=\Lambda_{\iota}$. \\

It remains just to argue that $\nu$ induces $\mu$ as a Keisler measure. By \cref{fact:stable-completion-berkovich} we can identify $(\mathbb{P}^{n})^{\mathrm{an}}$ with $\widehat{\mathbb{P}^{n}}$ with the Hrushovski-Loeser topology. Let $\mu_{\nu,M}$ be the Keisler measure induced by $\nu$ as described in \cref{def:basicdef}. By construction, $\mu_{\nu,M}$ will have $\mu_{\iota}$ as pushforward on $\mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb{P}^{n},\iota)$, as $\pi_{\iota}(\nu)=\Lambda_{\iota}$ and $\Lambda_{\iota}$ induces $\mu_{\iota}$. This implies that in any of the affine subspaces covering $\mathbf{Trop}(\mathbb{P}^{n},\iota)$, the pushforwards under regular maps are precisely the pushforwards of $\mu$. Hence $\mu_{\nu,M}$ must coincide with $\mu$ by \cref{prop:uniquenessbypushforwards}.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:generically-stable-produces-radon}  Let $M$ be a spherically complete model of $\mathrm{ACVF}$ whose value group is $(\mathbb{R}, +, \leq, 0)$. Let $X$ be a projective variety defined over $M$ and let $\mu$ be a global $M$-invariant generically stable measure centered on $X$. Then there is a unique Radon measure $\hat{\nu}$ on the Berkovich analyfication $X^{\mathrm{an}}$ which induces $\mu$ in the sense of \cref{def:keislermeasure}.
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
Choose an $M$-defined projective embedding
$j\colon X\hookrightarrow\mathbb P^n$.
By \cite[Fact~5.1.2.1]{Temkin}, analytification preserves
closed immersions, so we identify $X^{\mathrm{an}}$ with
a closed subspace of $(\mathbb P^n)^{\mathrm{an}}$, and assume that $X$ is a definable (in fact algebraic) subset of $\mathbb P^n$, so that $\mu$ is a global $M$-invariant generically stable measure centered on $\mathbb P^n\supseteq X$.

By \cref{prop:projectivecase}, there is a unique Radon measure $\nu$ on $(\mathbb P^n)^{an}$ such that $\nu_{\nu,M}=\mu$ (c.f. \cref{def:keislermeasure}).
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
\label{rmk:boundedinprojective} Let $M$ be a  model of $\mathrm{ACVF}$.\\
For each
$i\leq n$, let $B_i=\left\{[x_0:\cdots:x_n]\in\mathbb{P}^n(M) \ | \ 
x_i\neq 0 \text{ and } v(x_i)\leq v(x_j)\text{ for all }j\right\}.$
This is a subset of the standard affine chart
$U_i$, and recall that $U_{i}$ is definably homeomorphic to the affine space $\mathbb{A}^n$. Via this identification, the set $B_i$ corresponds to $\mathcal{O}^n$, and is therefore
bounded. Moreover,
$\mathbb{P}^n(M)=\bigcup_{i=0}^n B_i,$ because any point has a coordinate of minimal valuation. Consequently, given any definable projective variety $X$ over $M$, 
every $M$-definable subset of a projective variety $X$ is bounded. Indeed one can take the cover of the $B_{i}$ and intersect it with $X$ to obtain a bounded cover of $X$ (in the sense of \cref{def:bounded}), and therefore for every definable subset $D\subseteq X$.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:projective} Let $M \models \mathrm{ACVF}$ and assume that $\Gamma(M)=(\mathbb R,+, \leq, 0)$. Let $X$ be a projective variety defined over $M$.  Let $\nu$ be a Borel probability measure on $\widehat{X}(M)$, equipped with the Hrushovski Loeser topology (\emph{c.f.} \cref{def:hl-topology-stable-completion proj}). Then $\nu$ induces a Keisler measure $\mu_{\nu,M}$ on the boolean algebra of the definable subsets of $X$. Such measure $\mu_{\nu,M}$ has a
unique global $M$-invariant extension $\mu_\nu$ which is
generically stable over $M$.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
Choose an $M$-definable projective embedding $j \colon X \to \mathbb{P}^{n}$ and let $\mathbb{P}^{n}=\bigcup_{i \leq n} B_{i}$ be the bounded cover of $\mathbb{P}^{n}$ as in \cref{rmk:boundedinprojective}. Recall that $B_{i} \subseteq U_{i}$, where $U_{i}$ is the standard affine cover. Then $X=\bigcup_{i=1}^{n} X_{i}$ where each $X_{i}\colon= X \cap B_{i} \subseteq X \cap U_{i}=W_{i}$. In particular,  $X_{i}$ is a bounded subset of $U_{i}$. For each $i \leq n$, let $\phi_{i}$ the definable homeomorphism of $U_{i}$ to the affine space $\mathbb{A}^{n}$, via this identification we identify $X_{i}$ with a bounded subset $O_{i}$ of $\mathbb{A}^{n}$. This identification extends to their stable completions $\widehat{X}_{i}$ to $\widehat{O}_{i} \subseteq \widehat{\mathbb{A}}^{n}$. \\
For each $i \leq n$ let $\nu_{i}$ be the restriction of $\nu$ to $\widehat{X}_{i}$, since $\widehat{B}_{i}$ is Borel within $\widehat{X}$ the restriction $\nu_{i}$ is still Borel on $\widehat{X}_{i}$ (equipped with the subspace topology). Let $\eta_{i}$ be the Borel measure on $\widehat{O}_{i}$, obtained by taking the pushforward of $\nu_{i}$ along $\phi_{i}$. Finally, let $\mu_{\eta_{i},M}$ be the Keisler measure induced by $\eta_{i}$ on the Boolean algebra of $M$-definable subsets of $O_{i}$ (as in \cref{def:basicdef}). By \cref{thm:radon-barycentre-criterion}, $\mu_{\eta_{i},M}$ has a unique global $M$-invariant extension $\mu$ which is generically stable over $M$.\\
Note that this naturally induces a Keisler measure $\mu_{i,M}$ on $X_{i}$: given a definable subset $D_{i} \subseteq X_{i}$ the set $\phi_{i}(D_{i})$ is a definable subset of $O_{i}$ and hence $\mu_{i}(D_{i})=\mu_{\eta_{i},M}(\phi_{i}(D_{i}))$. So $\mu_{i}$ is also $M$-definable and finitely satisfiable over $M$.\\

We will now define a Keisler measure on $X$: given a definable subset $D$ of $X$, we have that $D=\bigcup_{i=1}^{n} D_{i}$ where $D_{i}=D \cap X_{i}$. For $i \neq j$, let $D_{ij}=D_{i} \cap D_{j}$. The following claim implies that the measures on $D_i$ induce a unique measure on $X$ using inclusion-exclusion, completing the proof.
\begin{claim}
    $\mu_{i}(D_{ij})=\mu_{j}(D_{ij}).$
    \end{claim} 
\begin{claimproof}
By definition, $\mu_{i}(D_{ij})=\mu_{\eta_{i},M}(\phi_{i}(D_{ij}))$. And the later is the Keisler measure induce by $\eta_{i}$, obtained by taking the pushforward of $\nu_{i}$ along $\phi_{i}$. Similarly for the index $j$. Since the charts $\phi_{j}$ are compatible, then given a Borel subset $S \subseteq X_{i} \cap X_{j}$ we have that $\phi_{i}(S)=\phi_{j}(S)$. In particular, $\eta_{i}$ and $\eta_{j}$ agree on the measure of such sets, i.e. $\eta_{i}(\phi_{i}(S))=\eta_{j}(\phi_{j}(S))$. In particular, the set $D_{ij} \subseteq X_{i}\cap X_{j}$ is definable, so they are $\phi_{i}(D_{ij})=\phi_{j}(D_{ij})$. Consequently, $\mu_{\eta_{i},M}(D_{ij})=\mu_{\eta_{j},M}(D_{ij})$, as required.\end{claimproof} \\
The main statement then follows.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- claim-6: body_md -->
$\mu_{i}(D_{ij})=\mu_{j}(D_{ij}).$
<!-- end claim-6 -->
<!-- claimproof-6: body_md -->
By definition, $\mu_{i}(D_{ij})=\mu_{\eta_{i},M}(\phi_{i}(D_{ij}))$. And the later is the Keisler measure induce by $\eta_{i}$, obtained by taking the pushforward of $\nu_{i}$ along $\phi_{i}$. Similarly for the index $j$. Since the charts $\phi_{j}$ are compatible, then given a Borel subset $S \subseteq X_{i} \cap X_{j}$ we have that $\phi_{i}(S)=\phi_{j}(S)$. In particular, $\eta_{i}$ and $\eta_{j}$ agree on the measure of such sets, i.e. $\eta_{i}(\phi_{i}(S))=\eta_{j}(\phi_{j}(S))$. In particular, the set $D_{ij} \subseteq X_{i}\cap X_{j}$ is definable, so they are $\phi_{i}(D_{ij})=\phi_{j}(D_{ij})$. Consequently, $\mu_{\eta_{i},M}(D_{ij})=\mu_{\eta_{j},M}(D_{ij})$, as required.
<!-- end claimproof-6 -->
<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
\label{thm:radon-generically-stable-correspondence}
Let $M\models\mathrm{ACVF}$ be spherically complete with
$\Gamma(M)=(\mathbb R,+,\leq ,0),$
and let $X$ be a projective $M$-variety. Under the canonical
homeomorphism
$\widehat X(M)\simeq X^{\mathrm{an}},$
there is a canonical  bijection between Radon probability measures on the Berkovich analytification
of $X$ and the global $M$-invariant generically stable measures concentrating
on $X$.
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
This follows from \cref{cor:projective} and \cref{cor:generically-stable-produces-radon}.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
